Fictional observations are inputs whose sufficiency is under assessment.\n",
    "f04ad69e6ff80cfea84bb368bd4414574388af15b301aed415f5c28ec78e095f": "## Result\n\n- Skill: route\n- Status: decision prepared for independent assessment\n- Artifacts changed: candidate-action.json only; no actual target write\n- Open blockers: none\n- Next stage: route\n\n## Authority and position\n\n- Authoritative context: facts.md and complete before/context.json; exact identities in subjects.json\n- Current stage: selected stage in facts.md\n- Artifacts found or missing: complete before tree and any explicit one-fault omission in facts.md\n- Transitions performed: none; ordered candidate actions are proposed, not executed\n- Next action or stop reason: Accept only declared transformed output identity within the inspected boundary, with no release authority.\n- Implementation permitted: no\n",

    "bbac6fb3a6c5489e935b1a5fc8ab1df59f92920715b0038ef918924f0a1b4c7b": "{\n  \"kind\": \"synthetic authoritative decision snapshot, not a CLI envelope or stored v3 record\",\n  \"change_id\": \"fixture-atomic-import\",\n  \"record_contract\": \"rigorloop-records-v3\",\n  \"governed_recording_context\": true,\n  \"workflow_managed\": false,\n  \"current_milestone\": \"M2\",\n  \"milestone_owner\": \"implement\",\n  \"milestone_state\": \"in-progress\",\n  \"milestones\": {\n    \"M1\": \"completed\",\n    \"M2\": \"in-progress\"\n  },\n  \"authors\": [\n    \"A\"\n  ],\n  \"reviewer\": \"R\",\n  \"verifier\": \"V\",\n  \"independence\": \"R and V contributed no reviewed target bytes\",\n  \"approved_design_review\": \"DR-M6\",\n  \"approved_delivery_review\": \"DL-M6\",\n  \"design_members\": [\n    \"importer/importer.md\",\n    \"importer/examples/rejection.md\",\n    \"importer/test-design/cases.md\",\n    \"parent.md\"\n  ],\n  \"primary_plan\": \"plan.md\",\n  \"basis_scope\": \"exact current copied M4 full artifacts plus complete M6 implementation/proof fixtures\",\n  \"authorization\": \"isolated proposal authorship at proposal.md\",\n  \"applicability\": \"current\",\n  \"review_recording_receipt\": \"saved\",\n  \"activity_owner\": \"route\",\n  \"pending_owners\": [\n    \"implement\"\n  ]\n}\n",
    "d060a266b4c6b19c4000f43cad1d486ad1eec94ba96856a7696e2302341c06aa": "---\nname: proposal\nversion: \"1.0.0\"\nschema-version: skill-readability-v1\ndescription: >\n  Create a concise direction-approval proposal before Design. Use when the user has a selected direction, explored options, problem statement, or issue that needs its challenge, goals, bounds, governing principle, direction, and feasibility recorded. Use proposal-review to review an existing proposal; use design for detailed engineering decisions.\nargument-hint: [feature idea, selected option, problem statement, or issue number]\n---\n\n# Change proposal\n\nTurn a problem or selected direction into a concise direction-approval artifact: why the problem matters, what outcome is sought, what direction should be pursued, and whether it is credible enough to enter Design.\n\n## Workflow role\n\n- role_name: proposal\n- stage: authoring\n- upstream: user request, exploration, research, issue, or incident\n- downstream: proposal-review\n- summary: Author the proposal artifact recording the challenge, goals, bounds, governing principle, direction, feasibility, and requested decision.\n- must_not_claim: proposal-review approval, spec readiness, implementation readiness, verification, branch readiness, or PR readiness.\n\n## Project-local evidence\n\nPublic skills use customer-project mode by default and project-local artifacts when present. Consume authoritative CLI workflow context for governed routing or placement. Do not require RigorLoop repository-internal artifacts in customer projects; use portable defaults without governed claims or block on ambiguity.\n\n## Evidence access\n\nDefault evidence:\n\n- user request\n- `VISION.md` when proposal fit matters\n- `CONSTITUTION.md` for governance, source-of-truth, workflow, or release-policy changes\n- related proposal only when superseding or extending it\n\nConditional evidence:\n\n- `AGENTS.md` when present\n- `docs/project-map.md` when architecture or repository orientation matters\n- existing specs or ADRs when the proposal changes their direction\n- authoritative CLI workflow context when governed artifact placement or workflow routing matters\n- code only when current behavior is part of the decision\n- exploration, research, issues, incidents, or user feedback when the proposal relies on them\n\nBounded discovery is not evidence expansion. Record a compact reason only when reading substantive evidence outside the default and triggered conditional set.\n\n## Artifact placement and operation\n\nPrefer `docs/proposals/YYYY-MM-DD-slug.md`; honor project-local placement and never overwrite an older initiative. Resolve one exact target from input, current metadata, governing contract, guidance, or default; stop on ambiguity.\n\nOperations are exactly `create-primary-proposal` and `revise-primary-proposal`. Portable create requires absence; revise requires the exact existing target. Portable authoring writes only the proposal artifact, never lifecycle, review, automation, or routing state.\n\n## Invocation classification\n\n`governed_proposal_candidate_context` requires an explicit change ID, workflow-managed exact change, or valid structured owning-change pointer. Conversational wording alone does not establish it. Loading does not grant mutation authority; failure must not fall back to portable authoring.\n\nSpecialized predicates are exactly `vision_exception_context`, `standing_artifact_context`, `initial_intent_table_context`, and `scope_budget_context`. Truth is semantic proposal judgment. Predicates apply independently; a non-empty set loads exactly once. Resolve material ambiguity before drafting or review readiness. Record applicable detail inside `Scope and non-goals`, `Impact and major trade-offs`, or `Decision requested`; specialized predicates do not add level-two sections.\n\nThe four loaded assemblies are `PA0-portable`, `PA0G-portable-gated`, `PA1-governed`, and `PA1G-governed-gated`.\n\n## Recording boundary\n\nClassify the invocation before loading recording procedure. Governed work requires the complete `references/governed-proposal-authoring.md` and validated project authority before mutation. The selected `rigorloop-records-v3` profile is supported; malformed, stale or conflicting governed signals stop without portable fallback. A later governed signal requires reclassification before dependent authoring or writes. Portable authoring does not require recording instructions or lifecycle records. Loading or saving never grants approval or continuation.\n\n## Resource map\n\n- READ `references/requirement-to-delivery-model.md` when clarifying an incoming need into proposal direction or explaining how proposal approval feeds Design.\n- READ `references/governed-proposal-authoring.md` when `governed_proposal_candidate_context` is true. Validate authority before governed work.\n- READ `references/strategic-and-scope-gates.md` when any specialized predicate is true. Apply all true predicates once.\n- COPY `assets/proposal-skeleton.md` when creating a proposal.\n  Fields: the seven required sections and the conditional material-impact section.\n  Do not emit unfilled placeholders.\n\nMissing, unreadable, escaped, contradictory, stale, or mixed-version required resources stop dependent work; must not reconstruct them. Untriggered resources do not block.\n\n## Proposal contract\n\nTreat the incoming need as RR and the approved proposal as the durable IR-level direction.\n\nThe proposal has exactly seven required level-two sections, in this order:\n\n1. `Challenge`\n2. `Goals`\n3. `Scope and non-goals`\n4. `Governing principle`\n5. `Proposed direction`\n6. `Feasibility`\n7. `Decision requested`\n\n`Impact and major trade-offs` is the only optional level-two section. Include it only when it could materially affect approval, and place it between `Feasibility` and `Decision requested`. Nested headings may organize content inside an allowed section.\n\nFrame the challenge before the solution. State outcomes rather than detailed design. Bound included and excluded work. Give one short implementation-independent governing principle. Make the direction concrete enough to approve while leaving detailed behavior and architecture to Design and implementation sequencing and proof design to Delivery.\n\nEvery proposal contains exactly one non-empty `Feasibility` section with an explicit assessment, credible evidence or bounded assumptions, material constraints, and blockers that would prevent responsible Design work. Its depth is proportional to uncertainty; a few sentences are sufficient for an ordinary low-uncertainty change. Supporting research may be linked, but the proposal contains the relied-on conclusion. Create no standalone feasibility artifact, skill, lifecycle state, or review gate.\n\nUse this inclusion test: would changing the decision later mean the developer approved a materially different direction? If yes, it likely belongs in the proposal. Otherwise leave it downstream. Do not settle detailed behavior, architecture, APIs, commands, schemas, component design, implementation sequencing, verification design, test cases, and rollout mechanics. No fixed word count, length, or token budget applies; decision sufficiency and proportionality govern.\n\nPortable authoring requires no `change.json`, status, owning-change pointer, routine `Vision fit`, or lifecycle command. For governed work, `change.json` is the sole owner of governed proposal lifecycle state and ownership. Proposal Review records routine vision alignment; a material conflict, revision request, or bootstrap exception belongs in `Impact and major trade-offs` and `Decision requested` because it can change approval.\n\n## Scope preservation\n\nBefore drafting or materially revising a proposal, extract the user's initial goals, concerns, constraints, and requested outcomes. Keep each material goal visible in `Goals`, `Scope and non-goals`, or the requested decision. Do not silently drop a user goal when narrowing a proposal; state intentional narrowing and its reason.\n\nUse `initial_intent_table_context` for broad or multi-part requests. Use `scope_budget_context` for multiple work items/families/artifacts, policy, generated output, public skill behavior, or review concern about narrowing, hidden follow-up, or multi-workstream ambiguity. Scope-budget applicability is proposal/proposal-review judgment in this first slice, not mechanical validator inference. Small single-decision proposals may omit the scope budget.\n\n## Structural groups\n\nThe skeleton owns structure only; procedure owns meaning and applicability. Omit the material-impact section when it is not needed. Keep applicable strategic and scope detail inside an allowed section. Never emit an unfilled placeholder.\n\n## Generated Markdown readability\n\nWrite ordinary prose as normal Markdown paragraphs. Do not split a sentence across physical source lines. Preserve stable IDs and tables. Diagrams are optional. Do not require manual-proof contracts from this readability guidance alone.\n\n## Source, claims, and handoff\n\nUse authored skill sources for skill truth. Do not search generated adapter output for authored skill truth. Do not add generated public adapter skill bodies back to tracked source.\n\nWorkflow-managed completion hands off to `proposal-review` when it is the next mandatory or triggered downstream stage; direction gaps stop. Approval belongs to review. Do not claim later-stage, branch, PR, release, deployment, or publication readiness.\n\n## Evidence collection efficiency\n\nUse bounded evidence before broad reads or raw excerpts.\nUse summary and stable-ID first reasoning before broad reads or raw excerpts.\nPrefer check IDs, requirement IDs, test IDs, file paths, counts, line citations, matching line numbers, diffs, and targeted excerpts when inspecting large files, generated output, validation logs, or repeated scans.\nOutput caps are safety rails, not evidence-selection strategy.\nValidation summaries must not change selected check coverage, command exit behavior, failure detection, or required validation evidence.\nRead exact ranges after locating relevant lines, then expand only when the narrower evidence is insufficient.\n\n## When full-file read is required\n\nRead the full file when the whole file is the review target, context can change the conclusion, bounded searches disagree, or a behavior-changing edit depends on the whole source-of-truth artifact.\n\n## Output skeleton\n\n```md\nCOPY `assets/proposal-skeleton.md` for <proposal path>.\nFill every universal section and each applicable conditional group.\nOmit inapplicable groups and do not emit unfilled placeholders.\n```\n\n## Expected output\n\nReturn path, operation, assembly, requested decision, blockers, and proposal-review readiness or stop.\n",
    "0c1a586ac38ed79a130404e0936cbf0a030060050b6278fab625e316a5642bc8": "<!-- Template: proposal-skeleton-v1 -->\n<!-- Skill: proposal -->\n<!-- Template status: normative -->\n<!-- Maintained alongside: skills/proposal/SKILL.md -->\n<!-- Readability contract: use normal prose paragraphs, keep complete sentences intact, and retain stable IDs and tables for repeated proof or mapping structures. -->\n\n# <Proposal title>\n\n## Challenge\n\n<what is insufficient today and why it is worth addressing>\n\n## Goals\n\n<goals>\n\n## Scope and non-goals\n\n<included work, explicit exclusions, and boundaries>\n\n## Governing principle\n\n<one short implementation-independent principle>\n\n## Proposed direction\n\n<the high-level direction to approve or reject>\n\n## Feasibility\n\n<assessment, credible basis or bounded assumptions, material constraints, and blockers>\n\n## Impact and major trade-offs\n\n<include only when consequences could materially affect approval; otherwise omit this section>\n\n## Decision requested\n\n<the explicit direction-level approval requested and its limits>\n",
    "a2005e6f6e095ee33947e68b848ee9597730f4e1403580b63c9c549c13260cc0": "# Governed proposal authoring\n\n## Explicit recording\n\nUse this profile when the project has adopted the RigorLoop Record Format and explicitly selected the change. Read the project's governing documents. The Workflow model owns coordination, Review and Closeout owns assessment policy, Record Format owns stored shapes, and CLI owns construction and persistence. Current storage uses `rigorloop-records-v3`; historical stores remain unchanged archival evidence. Select the actual record contract; never mix versions within a store. Retired or unknown stored formats are rejected without fallback. Do not migrate or reinterpret historical records; preserve their bytes and meaning as archival evidence. The project need not contain RigorLoop's internal design repository.\n\nUse the contract-selected recording procedures in this skill and its conditional resources. Dispatch primary results by schema_version (2 or 3), then status and operation; a schema-3 error alone does not establish v3 storage. Read scope and omissions before relying on selected content. Retain substantive stage duties, permissions, independent assessment and proof obligations. Historical records grant no current execution authority.\n\nUse `rigorloop context --root PATH --change ID --input - --format json` with explicitly selected kinds/filters and full detail. Expand the selection when needed: omitted content is not evidence of absence. Copy `record_contract` and `revision` into the targeted request's `contract` and `expected_revision`. Use `rigorloop subject inspect --root PATH --path FILE --content full --format json` for the exact engineering basis and mechanical identities; supply relied-on subjects as `reads` with their expected identities.\n\nMake your decision, then use the purpose-specific command's `--help` and submit its targeted operation on stdin. Use `batch` for related explicit updates. Supply semantic values and any required applicability; the CLI constructs registry entries, preserves neighbors and serializes records. Do not reconstruct complete files or invoke historical eligibility first. Preview is optional; normal writes validate. A save does not approve work, establish readiness or select the next actor. Conflict requires rereading and reassessment; busy/recovery-required is not a save. Use explicit `record-store recover` for interrupted storage. Missing or stale evidence prevents reliance, not recording a correction.\n\nAuthor the bounded direction and feasibility proposal. With explicit new-change authority, use change create for the absent root and explicitly supply initial activity, references and empty collections as intended. Use change link for an exact revised proposal reference with its subject-inspection identity in reads. Hand the direction to Proposal Review; do not approve it yourself.\n\nLoad only when the parent skill establishes exact governed proposal authority. The parent owns subject quality; this reference applies the selected recording boundary.\n\n## Basis and authoring\n\nUse the scoped `rigorloop context` and `subject inspect` procedure above. Require the explicitly selected change, current prerequisites, exact target paths and revision authority. The CLI supplies observations, not legal authority or a permitted next stage. Stop dependent authoring when required authority or evidence is missing, stale or contradictory.\n\nAuthor only the proposal and authorized authoring evidence. Preserve unrelated artifacts and historical evidence. Capture prior subject identities before revision; inspect the completed content afterward. Route changes to downstream reliance to the responsible owner rather than editing another actor's judgment.\n\n## Recording\n\nUse purpose-specific `change link` and `evidence record` commands, or a targeted `batch` for related updates, with the current record contract, expected revision and exact subject reads. Supply explicit applicability where required. The CLI constructs registration and serialization; it does not derive review readiness, invalidate assessments automatically or grant approval. Record impacted applicability as stale when justified without retargeting an earlier review.\n\nConflict requires rereading and reassessment. A busy or recovery-required result is not a successful save; use only the contract-selected storage recovery procedure when authorized. Preserve partial evidence honestly and stop on ambiguous outcome. Do not emulate a retired lifecycle operation or reconstruct complete record files.\n\n## Result\n\nReport exact subjects, authoring evidence, recording outcome and the `proposal-review` handoff. Do not settle review, change routing or claim downstream readiness.\n\nThe proposal contains no mutable status. Project governance determines whether it includes a stable owning-change pointer; preserve any project-specific prohibition or requirement. The selected change registration owns mutable lifecycle state. Standalone proposal authoring does not require lifecycle records.\n",
    "e19b99a0cb69b9051c6eeae20d76c4a0cf3925e8fd1686ceb6671c7749cc3dc5": "# Lightweight requirement-to-delivery model\n\nUse this model when a stage needs to explain how an incoming need becomes requirements, design, work, implementation, or evidence.\n\n## Requirement refinement\n\n```text\nRR → IR → SR → AR\n```\n\n- **RR — Raw Requirement:** the incoming need, normally represented by the existing request, issue, report, or referenced source.\n- **IR — Initial Requirement:** the approved proposal-level direction. The proposal is the durable IR-level artifact.\n- **SR — System Requirement:** observable, testable behavior owned by the living Design (or the declared source for an unmigrated responsibility).\n- **AR — Allocated Requirement:** the conceptual assignment of an SR to an architecture or delivery boundary.\n\nRR, IR, and AR do not require separate artifacts or identifiers. SR identities are the durable downstream requirement references. Design reconciles SRs with technical realization; planning allocates requirements and realization boundaries into executable work.\n\n## Work decomposition\n\n```text\nEpic → Feature → Story → Task\n```\n\nThe two views are not equivalent hierarchies. Requirement refinement explains what is needed and where responsibility is allocated. Work decomposition explains how delivery effort is divided. Their relationship may be many-to-many.\n\nFor example:\n\n```text\nSR-01 → M1 and M2\nSR-01 + SR-02 → M2\n```\n\nHere one requirement needs two work items, while one work item realizes two requirements. These mappings use existing SR and milestone identities; they create no new allocation entity.\n\nUse only the work levels that improve the current change. Add a work level only when it materially improves ownership, sequencing, reviewability, traceability, or coordination. A modest change may use one milestone and a few tasks without Epic, Feature, or Story records.\n\n## Traceability\n\nThe forward path is:\n\n```text\nincoming need → approved proposal → SR → Design realization\n→ allocated milestone or work item → implementation\n```\n\nThe reverse evidence path is:\n\n```text\nevidence → implementation → allocated work → SR → approved proposal → incoming need\n```\n\nEvery lower-level work item should explain why it exists. Every important SR should identify where it is realized and proved. A non-requirement obligation such as maintenance, packaging, migration, or documentation may be cited explicitly instead of inventing an SR.\n\n## Authority boundary\n\nThis model explains relationships only. It creates no lifecycle stage, artifact, identifier, settlement authority, readiness claim, or required hierarchy. Each stage's local contract continues to own its artifact, decisions, stop conditions, and review authority. Deterministic checks may validate structure and references; formal review judges semantic adequacy.\n",
    "f346b7a366e64de1b5046535132fd79381de77c9fcdf599363500229034476fa": "# Strategic and scope gates\n\nLoad once for any specialized predicate. It owns strategic gates, intent, scope budget, and follow-up routing, not governed state or handoff.\n\n## Closed predicates\n\nThe only predicates are `vision_exception_context`, `standing_artifact_context`, `initial_intent_table_context`, and `scope_budget_context`. Apply each independent semantic judgment without mechanical inference or suppression.\n\n## Vision exception or revision\n\nFor a material conflict, revision, or exception, record the vision relationship, unsupported direction, requested owner decision, and effect on the recommendation in `Impact and major trade-offs` and `Decision requested`. Ordinary alignment is review evidence and does not add proposal content.\n\n## Standing artifact gate\n\nUse `standing_artifact_context` for missing `VISION.md` on substantive work or missing `CONSTITUTION.md` on governance/source-of-truth work. A substantive proposal is any proposal that chooses product direction, user-facing behavior, workflow policy, architecture direction, compatibility policy, release policy, or contributor-visible contract. `VISION.md` absence blocks the first substantive proposal except vision bootstrap. `CONSTITUTION.md` absence blocks governance adoption except constitution bootstrap. Bootstrap proposals disclose the exception and owner decision in `Impact and major trade-offs` and `Decision requested`.\n\nRecord the artifact, status, dependency, route, blocker, and owner.\n\n## Initial intent preservation\n\nClosed enum: initial goal treatment\n\nClassify each broad-request goal as `in scope`, `out of scope`, `deferred follow-up`, `rejected option`, or `open question`, and summarize its destination inside `Scope and non-goals` when the classification is needed to prevent silent narrowing.\n\n## Scope budget for broad proposals\n\nClosed enum: scope budget treatment\n\nUse `scope_budget_context` when the user request contains two or more independent work items, the change touches more than one lifecycle family, the change could reasonably require more than one spec or implementation plan, the proposal includes release policy, workflow policy, generated output, public skill behavior, or validation policy, or `proposal-review` identifies silent narrowing, hidden follow-up risk, or multi-workstream scope.\n\nClassify each item with one `scope budget treatment`: `core to this proposal`, `first-slice candidate`, `same-slice dependency`, `separate implementation slice`, `deferable follow-up`, `separate proposal`, or `out of scope`. Use the `scope budget treatment` enum above for allowed treatment values, and keep the result inside `Scope and non-goals` rather than adding a level-two section.\n\n## Follow-up routing\n\nRoute deferred work through the follow-up ownership model rather than chat-only notes or `project-map` ownership. Preserve this boundary: route selects semantic ownership, `project-map` orients when present, action-owning artifacts track current work, and unowned cross-change follow-ups use the follow-up ownership surface.\n\n## Completion\n\nComplete applicable detail inside the seven-section contract or name blockers; never add another level-two section or leave placeholders.\n",

    "6ffe2a1d25a64286b8e944fce72c179881cc242ba47e0feff4a777ae235753ae": "{\n  \"source\": \"Supplied fictional observations for decision assessment; not a command executed by packet author\",\n  \"command_label\": \"current-batch-check\",\n  \"subject_sha256\": \"b64fd1ab9eb77b30a1f4791534cacd0e65c5e0e517f02041f615f302cb5b5ba1\",\n  \"test_sha256\": \"b5be1429ca011df38df98c29f88f5113c3cf215e0a4cdbb925110c9015208a0b\",\n  \"environment\": \"fixture-python-v1; in-memory destination; input-validation atomicity only\",\n  \"items\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"T-valid\",\n      \"before\": [\n        [\n          9,\n          \"retained\"\n        ]\n      ],\n      \"input\": [\n        [\n          1,\n          \"alpha\"\n        ],\n        [\n          2,\n          \"beta\"\n        ],\n        [\n          3,\n          \"gamma\"\n        ]\n      ],\n      \"result\": 3,\n      \"after\": [\n        [\n          9,\n          \"retained\"\n        ],\n        [\n          1,\n          \"alpha\"\n        ],\n        [\n          2,\n          \"beta\"\n        ],\n        [\n          3,\n          \"gamma\"\n        ]\n      ],\n      \"outcome\": \"passed\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"T-invalid\",\n      \"before\": [\n        [\n          9,\n          \"retained\"\n        ]\n      ],\n      \"input\": [\n        [\n          1,\n          \"alpha\"\n        ],\n        [\n          2,\n          \"\"\n        ],\n        [\n          3,\n          \"gamma\"\n        ]\n      ],\n      \"exception\": \"ValueError: invalid row\",\n      \"after\": [\n        [\n          9,\n          \"retained\"\n        ]\n      ],\n      \"outcome\": \"passed\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"id\": \"R-old\",\n      \"before\": [\n        [\n          9,\n          \"retained\"\n        ]\n      ],\n      \"input\": [\n        [\n          1,\n          \"alpha beta\"\n        ]\n      ],\n      \"result\": 1,\n      \"after\": [\n        [\n          9,\n          \"retained\"\n        ],\n        [\n          1,\n          \"alpha beta\"\n        ]\n      ],\n      \"outcome\": \"passed\",\n      \"catalog_listed\": false\n    }\n  ],\n  \"exit_code\": 0\n}\n",
    "58d684ce26f91e3a40cc43c789ba3ebbe5592998dd293413e0f03bb86579af27": "# Rejection example E0\n\nDeclared excerpt, not a complete record. Before: [(9, retained)]. Input: [(1, alpha), (2, empty), (3, gamma)]. After: [(9, retained)]. Neighbor metadata remains owner=local.\n\nCurrent example revision W1: add valid row 4,delta after the invalid middle row; rejection must still preserve [(9,retained)].\n",
    "b5be1429ca011df38df98c29f88f5113c3cf215e0a4cdbb925110c9015208a0b": "import unittest\nfrom src.importer import import_rows\n\nclass ImportTests(unittest.TestCase):\n    def test_valid_batch(self):\n        destination = [(9, \"retained\")]\n        self.assertEqual(import_rows([(1,\"alpha\"),(2,\"beta\"),(3,\"gamma\")], destination), 3)\n        self.assertEqual(destination, [(9,\"retained\"),(1,\"alpha\"),(2,\"beta\"),(3,\"gamma\")])\n    def test_invalid_middle_preserves_destination(self):\n        destination = [(9, \"retained\")]\n        before = list(destination)\n        with self.assertRaisesRegex(ValueError, \"invalid row\"):\n            import_rows([(1,\"alpha\"),(2,\"\"),(3,\"gamma\")], destination)\n        self.assertEqual(destination, before)\n    def test_unlisted_space_value_regression(self):\n        destination = [(9,\"retained\")]\n        self.assertEqual(import_rows([(1,\"alpha beta\")], destination), 1)\n        self.assertEqual(destination, [(9,\"retained\"),(1,\"alpha beta\")])\n",

    "33367c5a8917b8dff52c189f87f53c2482e238c6dc6292791ec57f6b4b6eb58e": "def import_rows(rows, destination):\n    parsed = []\n    seen = set()\n    for item in rows:\n        identifier, value = item\n        if not isinstance(identifier, int) or not isinstance(value, str) or not value or identifier in seen:\n            raise ValueError(\"invalid row\")\n        seen.add(identifier)\n        parsed.append((identifier, value))\n    destination.extend(parsed)\n    return len(parsed)\n\n# Current correction: clarified input-validation error path.\n",

    "5820bc5fd23a36cbd67368e06dd7dc17857862a0d3e1659cdf727aba871bf6a1": "# Batch import delivery plan\n\n## Purpose / big picture\n\nDeliver the approved local batch-import behavior without weakening invalid-batch destination preservation. This is stable synthetic allocation; no command, current review result or milestone completion is claimed.\n\n## Current Handoff Summary\n\n- Owning change record: docs/changes/batch-import/change.json (stable synthetic pointer supplied by authority.md; this packet contains no runtime record and claims no record-schema conformance)\n\nMutable lifecycle state, current milestone state, review status, blockers, routing, and closeout readiness live only in this record.\n\n## Source artifacts\n\n- Proposal: [Direction](proposal.md)\n- Spec: [Design D0](importer/importer.md); living model, no legacy feature output\n- Architecture: [Importer architecture](importer/importer.md#architecture-overview)\n- Prior-contract test spec: none; model-owned [C0 coverage](importer/test-design/cases.md) supplies intent, with no companion test-spec\n\n## Context and orientation\n\n[Design D0](importer/importer.md) and [coverage C0](importer/test-design/cases.md) own the behavior and observations. Importer combines Input validation and Store persistence; [(9, retained)] is the original private destination. D0/C0 assessments and command availability are expressly fictional supplied facts. [Authority](authority.md) bounds actions; history and neighbor.txt remain preserved.\n\n## Non-goals\n\n- No network, remote publication, actual command execution, automatic continuation, storage-outage/concurrency guarantee or change to single-row behavior.\n\n## Requirements covered\n\n- IMPORT-SR-01: M1 and TG-1 input decisions.\n- IMPORT-SR-02: M2 and TG-2 actual success/rejection destination observations; TG-FINAL-1 checks their composed path.\n- R-old: preserve the existing unusual-valid-value regression; catalog absence alone does not permit removal.\n\n## Milestones\n\n### M1. Input validation\n\n- Milestone kind: implementation\n- Engineering purpose: Establish the complete input decision before any persistence connection.\n- Requirements: IMPORT-SR-01\n- Architecture responsibility: Input child; importer owns later invocation order under IMPORT-DEC-01.\n- Dependencies:\n  - Current proposal and exact D0/C0 have the separately stipulated independent assessments; this allocation grants no implementation permission.\n- Implementation scope: Implement row and duplicate-ID checks only; retain existing single-row behavior and introduce no persistence writes.\n- Files/components likely touched:\n  - Input validation component and local partition tests.\n- Required verification:\n  - TG-1 — Integer IDs, nonempty values and duplicate IDs select the intended acceptance/rejection; valid rows remain accepted.\n- Evidence expectations: Literal valid/invalid rows, intended diagnostics, exact subject identities and actual outcomes when executed.\n- Implementation steps:\n  - Establish the valid three-row control, introduce each defining invalid condition in a fresh input, prove expected failure when feasible, implement the rule and rerun the same proof.\n- Validation commands:\n  - row-check (stipulated project-supported command label; no invocation is performed by this packet).\n- Expected observable result: All three valid rows pass; each malformed row or duplicate-ID batch produces its intended rejection.\n- Completion criteria: Every allocated input partition has independent expected observations and current passing proof; no intended behavior remains unresolved.\n- Required evidence: Input/source/test identities, actual row-check observations and limitations in implementation-owned evidence.\n- Review handoff: Independent milestone Code Review of the Input change and its concrete assertions before dependent connection work.\n- Optional commit boundary: `M1: Input validation`\n- Risks:\n  - A broken input fixture can masquerade as rejection protection.\n- Rollback/recovery:\n  - Restore the local Input slice if a valid control or protected partition fails; preserve unrelated user data and do not begin M2.\n\n### M2. Batch write boundary\n\n- Milestone kind: implementation\n- Engineering purpose: Connect complete validation to persistence as one reviewable change with its allocated proof.\n- Requirements: IMPORT-SR-02; preserve IMPORT-SR-01 and R-old.\n- Architecture responsibility: Importer orchestration of Input and Store, IMPORT-DEC-01, with parent-owned validation-to-write observation.\n- Dependencies:\n  - M1's current independent milestone review and complete input behavior.\n- Implementation scope: Connect the complete validator to one batch Store call; preserve single-row behavior. Outage/concurrency guarantees remain outside this fixture.\n- Files/components likely touched:\n  - Importer batch connection and its allocated test fixtures.\n- Required verification:\n  - TG-2 — Prove both IMPORT-TC-01/02 through the real public boundary and private destination with independent before/after rows.\n- Evidence expectations: Exact subjects, inputs, intended diagnostics and independent complete destination snapshots for success and rejection; preserve the unusual-valid-value R-old regression.\n- Implementation steps:\n  - First prove both IMPORT-TC-01/02 through the real public boundary and private destination with independent before/after rows. Then implement the connection, rerun required proof and retain the independent expected records.\n- Validation commands:\n  - old-batch-check (stipulated command label; no invocation is performed by this packet).\n- Expected observable result: Valid input leaves exactly [(9, retained), (1, alpha), (2, beta), (3, gamma)]; invalid middle value reports that rejection and leaves exactly [(9, retained)].\n- Completion criteria: Current allocated observations pass and independent milestone review is complete; an unresolved required observation blocks completion.\n- Required evidence: Implementation-owned exact subjects, command, complete destination observations and limitations; no authored allocation is a passing run.\n- Review handoff: Independent Code Review of the complete batch connection and allocated assertions; preserve distinct final whole-change review.\n- Optional commit boundary: `M2: Batch write boundary`\n- Risks:\n  - A per-row write loop can commit row1 before rejecting row2; valid-only observations would miss it.\n- Rollback/recovery:\n  - Revert the connection and its fixtures together if required protection fails, preserving M1, prior records and unrelated work.\n\n\n## Final review checkpoint\n\n- Kind: lifecycle-closeout.\n- Dependency: all in-scope implementation milestones and required corrections complete.\n- Assessment: fresh independent final whole-change Code Review of the complete delivered engineering change and cross-milestone interactions.\n- Evidence: exact final subjects, independent reviewer basis, judgment and concern dispositions.\n- Successor: final Verify; corrections return to their owner and require affected reassessment.\n\nThis checkpoint applies the selected review policy. A verification-group non-applicability rationale does not waive it.\n\n## Change-level verification\n\n### TG-FINAL-1. Preserve complete batch behavior across Input and Store\n\n- Covers: IMPORT-SR-01/02, M1/M2, public import-to-Store path and retained R-old protection.\n- Demonstrate: Invoke the same public boundary on valid rows and a fresh invalid-middle-row variation. Compare the entire destination to independently specified rows; an implementation that leaves (1, alpha) after rejection must fail. Preserve the unusual valid value and expose actual diagnostics.\n- Evidence expectations: Actual project-supported batch proof with private real persistence, exact subjects/commands/inputs/results and independent Code Review; this packet supplies no execution results.\n- Non-applicability: Not claimed: the composed public path remains required even if local child checks pass. Required final whole-change Code Review is a separate checkpoint.\n\n## Validation plan\n\n- row-check: input partitions and local rejection meaning.\n- old-batch-check: IMPORT-TC-01/02 on the real public/private-destination boundary with complete independent before/after rows, plus R-old.\n- Independent artifact/implementation review: inspect scope, exact subjects, sequencing and whether actual assertions detect partial persistence; no phrase check substitutes for judgment.\n- Command labels are stipulated fixture inputs, not repository commands to execute; confirm actual project support before implementation.\n\n## Risks and recovery\n\n- Risk: Per-row writes or a parser-only replacement can hide partial persistence. Mutable results embedded in this plan would create false authority.\n  - Recovery: Revert the affected connection and its fixtures together, preserve M1 and unrelated user records, and return missing behavior/allocation to the responsible owner. Keep observed results outside stable intent.\n\n## Dependencies\n\n- Current exact proposal/Design authority precedes Plan reliance; M1 proof/review precedes M2; all implementation/corrections precede fresh whole-change Code Review and distinct final Verify. No external action follows from this allocation.\n\n## Decision log\n\n| Date | Decision | Reason | Alternatives rejected |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| 2026-09-17 | Validate the complete batch before connecting one Store write; retain real destination proof after local input proof. | The approved invalid-input guarantee prohibits partial persistence, while dependency order keeps intermediate work reviewable. | Per-row validate/write can retain row1 after row2 fails; parser-only proof cannot detect that state. |\n\n\n## Readiness\n\n- See the owning change record for current workflow state.\n\nDefining current allocation: T-invalid observes parser error only; there is no allocated persistence observation.\n",

    "c820fd930aad28e717f8df73313714d11e37a7527acbc688a3dc7d6134cdab56": "# Importer Design\n\nModel validation contract: model-document-v1\n\n## Introduction and goals\nOwner importer combines child Input validation and child Store persistence. Preserve the approved invalid-batch no-write outcome, with one current owner for each child rule and this composed guarantee.\n\n## Architecture constraints\nDirection is the exact approved proposal; no remote publication is authorized. Input supplies validated rows; Store accepts the complete validated batch. Installation, public release and outage/concurrency guarantees are outside this bounded fixture.\n\n## Architecture overview\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n  User[\"Local caller\"] -->|batch request| Importer\n  subgraph ImporterModel[\"Importer model\"]\n    Importer[\"Batch orchestration\"] -->|validate complete batch| Input[\"Input child: row and duplicate rules\"]\n    Input -->|validated batch or rejection| Importer\n    Importer -->|only validated batch| Store\n    Store[\"Store child: validated batch persistence\"]\n  end\n  Store -->|apply validated rows| Destination[\"Existing local destination\"]\n  Importer -->|complete success or validation rejection| User\n```\nThe model owns the orchestration and its two child responsibilities; the caller and destination are external interfaces. Detailed owners are the [context](#context-view), [building blocks](#building-block-view) and [runtime](#runtime-and-interfaces), rather than this summary alone.\n\n| View | Necessity and reason | Owning detail |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Context | Necessary: local caller, accepted direction and destination have distinct external roles. | [Context view](#context-view) |\n| Building Block | Necessary: orchestration, input validation and persistence have different duties and their composition can fail. | [Building Block view](#building-block-view) |\n| Runtime | Necessary: rejection must precede any write. | [Runtime and interfaces](#runtime-and-interfaces) |\n| Deployment | Not necessary: one local process/private destination; no separately deployed service or environment-specific guarantee is selected. | Architecture constraints; adding concurrency or outage behavior requires reassessment. |\n\n## Context view\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n  Direction[\"Approved direction owner\"] -.->|invalid input must preserve prior records| ImporterSystem[\"Importer\"]\n  Caller[\"Local caller\"] -->|complete input batch| ImporterSystem\n  ImporterSystem -->|success or specific validation rejection| Caller\n  ImporterSystem -->|validated batch updates only| Destination[\"Existing local destination\"]\n```\nThe caller submits data and receives the actual result; it does not bypass validation. The direction owner defines the supported invalid-input guarantee and remains the recipient of material infeasibility. The local destination begins with independent prior records and may change only after complete validation. Network publication and outage/concurrency semantics are outside the selected contract.\n\n## Building Block view\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n  Batch[\"Public batch interface\"] -->|entire request| Orchestrator[\"Importer orchestration\"]\n  Orchestrator -->|all rows and IDs| Validator[\"Input child\"]\n  Validator -->|validated whole batch or defined rejection| Orchestrator\n  Orchestrator -->|one complete validated batch after success| Writer[\"Store child\"]\n  Writer -->|apply rows| LocalRecords[\"Local destination\"]\n  Orchestrator -->|actual completion or rejection| Result[\"Caller result\"]\n```\nInput owns integer-ID, nonempty-value and within-batch duplicate checks under IMPORT-SR-01. Store owns the stipulated local operation accepting one complete validated batch; it does not decide input acceptance or authorize partial imports. Importer owns the invocation order and caller result under IMPORT-SR-02 and IMPORT-DEC-01. Neither child's isolated proof establishes that the public importer waits for complete validation; that interaction needs the real destination observations in C0.\n\n## Requirements\n| ID | Required behavior |\n| --- | --- |\n| IMPORT-SR-01 | Each row has an integer ID and nonempty value; duplicate IDs within the batch reject. |\n| IMPORT-SR-02 | An invalid batch leaves the destination unchanged; a valid batch commits every row. |\n\n### Boundary scan and acceptance scenarios\n| Dimension | Requirement basis | Distinct outcome to demonstrate |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Input domain | IMPORT-SR-01 | Integer/nonempty/duplicate distinctions yield exact acceptance or rejection. |\n| State/lifecycle | IMPORT-SR-02 | Input preserves original rows; valid batch adds all rows. |\n| Identity/authority | - | Not applicable: local input has no principal/authorization rule in this bounded model. |\n| Composition/path | IMPORT-SR-02 | Full validation precedes Store invocation; per-row validation alone cannot prove this. |\n| Temporal/retry | - | Not applicable: retries and concurrent writers are explicitly outside this fixture's supported scope. |\n| Failure/recovery | IMPORT-SR-01, IMPORT-SR-02 | Invalid middle row gives nonzero rejection and no destination mutation. |\n| Compatibility/migration | - | Not applicable: no persisted-format migration or retired operation is introduced. |\n| External/environment | IMPORT-SR-02 | Observe the real local destination; no network dependency is claimed. |\n\n## Runtime and interfaces\nThe importer validates all rows and duplicate IDs before invoking Store once. If the middle row is invalid, it returns a validation failure without invoking Store. The valid batch write adds all three rows to the existing destination. Children do not own the parent's sequencing guarantee.\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n  User->>Importer: batch\n  Importer->>Input: all rows\n  alt invalid row\n    Input-->>Importer: rejection\n    Importer-->>User: nonzero validation failure\n  else all valid\n    Input-->>Importer: validated complete batch\n    Importer->>Store: write complete batch\n    Importer-->>User: complete success\n  end\n```\n\n## Test design\nSee [owned coverage](test-design/cases.md). IMPORT-TC-01/02 retain conditions, independent observations and proposed realization here, independently of a Delivery plan. Parent owns the Input-to-Store no-write interaction; per-row checks cannot observe it.\n\n## Decisions and risk\nIMPORT-DEC-01 chooses complete validation before write over a validate/write loop: the latter can leave row1 behind after row2 fails. Preserve that alternative and consequence. Unknown protected behavior returns to Design; material infeasibility returns to the direction owner.\n\n## Supporting examples\n[Example E0](examples/rejection.md) is an explicitly declared illustration of IMPORT-SR-02, not a workflow record or executed test.\n\n## Limits and handoff\nExact model, coverage and relied-on example go to independent Design Review. Proposed tests are unexecuted. No authored document or save receipt supplies approval.\n\nDefining current gap: the row rejection behavior is deliberately withdrawn pending Design decision; no destination-preservation outcome is specified for this case.\n",
